Actors/Google Trends Interest & Related Query Scraper

Google Trends Interest & Related Query Scraper

Get interest-over-time, interest-by-region, and related topics/queries for any keyword, location, time range and Google surface, with side-by-side comparison for up to five keywords.

Open on ApifyGitHub source

Input schema

Input fields, types, required flags, descriptions, defaults, and prefills come from the published definition. Prefill examples are distinct from defaults.

FieldTypeRequiredDescription
modestringoptionalWhat to scrape. 'Interest over time & related queries' is the Google Trends Explore view (keywords required). 'Trending now' lists what is spiking in a country right now.
searchTermsarrayoptionalOne or more search terms to analyze (mode=interest). Each term becomes its own dataset row, or enable Comparison mode to compare up to 5 terms side by side. Example: `tesla` or `tesla, ford, toyota`.
compareModebooleanoptionalCompare all search terms on one shared 0-100 scale in a single row (Google Trends comparison view, maximum 5 terms). Off means each term is scraped independently.
Show all input fields
FieldTypeRequiredDescription
modestringoptionalWhat to scrape. 'Interest over time & related queries' is the Google Trends Explore view (keywords required). 'Trending now' lists what is spiking in a country right now.
searchTermsarrayoptionalOne or more search terms to analyze (mode=interest). Each term becomes its own dataset row, or enable Comparison mode to compare up to 5 terms side by side. Example: `tesla` or `tesla, ford, toyota`.
compareModebooleanoptionalCompare all search terms on one shared 0-100 scale in a single row (Google Trends comparison view, maximum 5 terms). Off means each term is scraped independently.
startUrlsarrayoptionalPaste Google Trends links to scrape them directly: explore links (trends.google.com/trends/explore?q=..., including www.google.com/trends/... redirect links) and embedded-chart links (trends.google.com/trends/embed/explore/...?req=...). Trending Now links (trends.google.com/trending?geo=...) are supported too. URL settings override the fields above; they are merged with your search terms.
geostringoptionalCountry, state/region or metro area to analyze. Leave empty for worldwide. Trending Now uses the country part of this location.
timeRangestringoptionalTime window for the interest data (mode=interest). 'now' ranges return minute-level points; 'today' ranges return day/week points. Choose 'Custom range' to enter exact dates below.
customDateRangestringoptionalOnly used when 'Time range' = 'Custom range': 'YYYY-MM-DD YYYY-MM-DD' (e.g. '2024-01-01 2024-12-31') or 'YYYY-MM-DDTHH YYYY-MM-DDTHH' for hourly granularity.
categorystringoptionalGoogle Trends category filter (mode=interest). 0 = All categories.
searchTypestringoptionalWhich Google surface to query (mode=interest). Web Search is the default.
geoResolutionstringoptionalBreakdown level for 'interest by region' (mode=interest). Auto lets Google pick the right level for the location; other levels are applied when the location supports them.
trendingHoursstringoptionalHow far back the Trending Now list goes (mode=trending).
trendingCategorystringoptionalFilter the Trending Now list to one of Google's trending categories (mode=trending).
includeTrendingNewsbooleanoptionalAttach news headlines, links and sources from Google's Trending RSS feed to trending items whose titles it covers (about ten highlighted stories per country, anywhere in the list).
maxItemsintegeroptionalMaximum dataset records per run: each interest row counts as one record, each trending item as one record. Remaining work stops once the limit is reached.

Download the published input schema

Key-value schema

Published key-value store definitions, when provided by this build.

This definition is not present in the default published build. See the published README for available documentation.

Published README

Read the full published documentation

Google Trends Interest & Related Query Scraper

Pull Google Trends data without an API key: interest over time, interest by region, related queries, and the Trending Now feed — for any keyword, location, time window and Google surface, cookielessly and with no proxy setup.

What this actor does

  • Interest mode returns the same Explore view you see on trends.google.com: a 0-100 interest timeline, an interest-by-region breakdown, related topics (when Google serves them) and top and rising related queries, plus derived metrics (averageInterest, latestInterest, peakInterest, peakDate, trendChangePercent, trendDirection).
  • Comparison mode puts up to five terms on one shared 0-100 scale and also returns per-term region breakdowns and per-term related queries, so you can see how each term ranks.
  • Trending mode returns what is spiking in a country right now: rank-ordered items with the Google-reported volume bucket, growth percentage, start time and related terms, optionally with matching news headlines and links. Filter by any of Google's 18 trending categories.
  • Works with any country plus 3,400+ states, regions and metros, all nine preset time windows (including real-time now ranges) and custom date ranges down to the hour, 1,100+ categories, and all five Google surfaces (Web, Images, News, Shopping, YouTube).
  • Every row links straight back to Google Trends via exploreUrl or trendingUrl for a manual spot-check, and every row is tagged with recordType (interest or trending).

Input

Field Type Default Description
mode string (select) interest interest = Explore keyword analysis; trending = Trending Now for a country.
searchTerms array of strings – Keywords to analyze (mode=interest). Each term becomes its own row, or compare up to five together.
compareMode boolean false Compare all terms on one shared 0-100 scale in a single row (maximum 5 terms).
startUrls array of requests – Optional Google Trends links (explore, embedded-chart embed/explore and Trending Now URLs, including www.google.com/trends/... redirect links). URL settings override the fields above.
geo string (select) Worldwide Country, state/region or metro to analyze. Trending Now uses the country part.
timeRange string (select) today 12-m Interest time window: now 1-H … all, or custom with customDateRange.
customDateRange string – Used when timeRange is custom: YYYY-MM-DD YYYY-MM-DD or YYYY-MM-DDTHH YYYY-MM-DDTHH.
category string (select) 0 (All categories) Google Trends category filter (mode=interest).
searchType string (select) Web Search Google surface: Web, Images, News, Shopping or YouTube (mode=interest).
geoResolution string (select) Auto Breakdown level for interest by region; applied when the location supports it.
trendingHours string (select) 24 Trending window: past 4, 24 or 48 hours, or past 7 days (mode=trending).
trendingCategory string (select) All categories Filter trending items to one of Google's 18 trending categories (mode=trending).
includeTrendingNews boolean true Attach matching news headlines, links and sources to trending items (mode=trending).
maxItems integer (1-10000) 100 Cap on dataset records per run (one per interest row or trending item).

Example: one keyword over the last 12 months

{ "mode": "interest", "searchTerms": ["tesla"], "geo": "US", "timeRange": "today 12-m" }

Example: compare three brands worldwide over five years

{
  "searchTerms": ["tesla", "ford", "toyota"],
  "compareMode": true,
  "geo": "",
  "timeRange": "today 5-y",
  "category": "47"
}

Example: Trending Now in Germany, last 24 hours

{ "mode": "trending", "geo": "DE", "trendingHours": "24", "includeTrendingNews": true, "maxItems": 100 }

Example: scrape pasted Google Trends links

{
  "startUrls": [
    { "url": "https://trends.google.com/trends/explore?q=pizza&geo=US&date=today%203-m" },
    { "url": "https://trends.google.com/trending?geo=GB&hours=48" }
  ]
}

Output

Every row carries recordType, geo, geoName and scrapedAt.

Interest rows: searchTerms, timeRange, timeRangeLabel, category, categoryName, searchType, searchTypeLabel, exploreUrl, interestOverTime (points with date, formattedTime, value — or values[] per term in comparison mode — and isPartial on the current period), averageInterest, latestInterest, peakInterest, peakDate, trendChangePercent, trendDirection, interestByRegion, relatedTopics, relatedQueries. trendDirection compares the last quarter of the window with the first (rising at +10% or more, falling at -10% or less). Comparison rows add interestByRegionPerTerm and relatedQueriesPerTerm.

Trending rows: rank, title, hours, hoursLabel, startedAt, endedAt, volume, volumeText, growthPercent, relatedTerms, news (when includeTrendingNews is on), trendingUrl.

A query that Google cannot serve (no data for the keyword) is logged and skipped; empty fields are omitted rather than written as nulls.

FAQ

What do the 0-100 interest numbers mean? They are Google's relative interest scores, normalised to 100 at the peak of the requested window and location — never absolute search counts. Google does not publish absolute volumes; for Trending Now, volume is Google's own bucket estimate.

Do I need an API key, cookies or a proxy? No. The actor works anonymously with no setup; when a request is rate-limited it waits and retries on its own, and only switches connection path if a block persists.

Why is relatedTopics sometimes missing? Related topics come from a separate Google widget that currently returns no data for most queries on Google Trends itself; the field appears whenever Google serves any. Related queries are unaffected.

How many terms can I compare at once? Five, Google Trends' own limit. Additional terms are trimmed. To cover more keywords, run them independently (compareMode off) — each becomes its own row on its own 0-100 scale.

Does Trending Now work for states or cities? No. Google only publishes the trending list at country level; a state or metro location is mapped to its country.

Why would a niche keyword show a flat or sparse timeline? Google returns points only where it has enough search data. Very rare terms yield mostly empty values; the actor reports exactly what Google sends.

Why are trending news links only on some rows? Google's Trending RSS feed highlights about ten stories per country rather than every trending item, and those stories can sit anywhere in the Trending Now list. Small record caps may cover none of them; raise maxItems to cover more of the list.

What does maxItems count? One interest row counts as one record and each trending item as one record. The run stops cleanly once the limit is reached.

What are the limits of a custom date range? Google Trends data starts in 2004 (earlier dates are clamped to 2004), the end date cannot be in the future, and hourly ranges are limited to about seven days. Longer windows automatically switch to weekly or monthly points.

Is this affiliated with Google? No. This is an independent tool and is not affiliated with, endorsed by, or sponsored by Google.

Use cases

  • SEO and content planning — mine rising related queries and topics before writing new content.
  • Brand and market tracking — compare brands on one scale and see where interest is rising.
  • Launch monitoring — watch breaking Trending Now items with their growth and news context.
  • Market research — map demand across countries and sub-regions for a product or category.
  • Seasonality analysis — pull multi-year timelines to find recurring peaks for planning.

Use a schema-derived input and the Python standard library to start a run. An Apify account and token are required for execution.
